I didn't even realize I was stuck there doing it until my manager tapped my shoulder and asked if I was okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or anxious right before these episodes where you freeze up?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, the restaurant gets busy, sure, but I don't feel especially stressed or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really seem connected to that. \n\nIt's not like I'm overwhelmed by a big dinner rush or anything when it starts. I could just be doing my normal routine, walking to the back or grabbing a glass, and it just... happens. I haven't really noticed any kind of warning or anxious feeling beforehand.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything like that. It's more just... I'm there, and then suddenly I'm just kind of stuck, and I don't realize I'm stuck until someone snaps me out of it. But I'm always just in my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... it's not like I feel like someone else is making me do things or moving my body for me. When I get stuck like that at work, I'm just paused. I'm not doing things against my will or anything. I just kind of stop doing much of anything at all until someone gets my attention. So, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember everything that happens before and after one of these episodes where you freeze up?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I remember everything before and after. Like... I know exactly what I was doing right up until I pause. I'll remember walking over to a table or picking up a tray. And then, once someone snaps me out of it, I know exactly where I am and what's going on. I just... I guess I just lose the time while I'm actually frozen. But the before and after are completely clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any memory lapses or gaps in your daily life where you couldn't recall what happened?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, just the time I lose when I'm actually frozen, like I mentioned. But other than that, no. My memory is completely fine. I don't have gaps in my day or forget conversations or anything like that. I pretty much remember everything normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or the world around you when you're not having one of these freezing episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, when I'm not stuck like that, I feel completely normal. I don't feel disconnected or... or like things aren't real. I'm just going about my day, working, doing whatever I normally do. It's really just those times when I freeze up that are weird. But otherwise, no, I feel perfectly connected to everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a kind of trance when you're frozen like that?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it a trance, really. I just... I don't feel anything at all. It's like I'm just paused. I'm not daydreaming or zoned out in some other world, I'm just not there for a minute. Then someone touches my shoulder and I'm back. So no, not really a trance. Just a blank spot.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF44.5, F43.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... things have been getting weird at work, and my boss said I needed to see someone. I'm a waitress, and lately I've just been... well, what scared everyone is that I started freezing up right in the middle of a shift. Like, I'd just stop moving. Or I'd do things totally out of order. The other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, and I was just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. I didn't really realize I was doing it until they pointed it out, but it's been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember what was going through your mind when you were standing by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer and repeating table numbers?\n\nPatient: Honestly... no, I don't. It's hard to explain, but I don't really remember thinking anything at all. It was just... blank, I guess? Like my brain just kind of paused. \n\nI wasn't trying to memorize the numbers or anything like that, they were just coming out of my mouth. I didn't even realize I was stuck there doing it until my manager tapped my shoulder and asked if I was okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or anxious right before these episodes where you freeze up?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, the restaurant gets busy, sure, but I don't feel especially stressed or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really seem connected to that. \n\nIt's not like I'm overwhelmed by a big dinner rush or anything when it starts. I could just be doing my normal routine, walking to the back or grabbing a glass, and it just... happens. I haven't really noticed any kind of warning or anxious feeling beforehand.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything like that. It's more just... I'm there, and then suddenly I'm just kind of stuck, and I don't realize I'm stuck until someone snaps me out of it. But I'm always just in my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... it's not like I feel like someone else is making me do things or moving my body for me. When I get stuck like that at work, I'm just paused. I'm not doing things against my will or anything. I just kind of stop doing much of anything at all until someone gets my attention. So, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember everything that happens before and after one of these episodes where you freeze up?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I remember everything before and after. Like... I know exactly what I was doing right up until I pause. I'll remember walking over to a table or picking up a tray. And then, once someone snaps me out of it, I know exactly where I am and what's going on. I just... I guess I just lose the time while I'm actually frozen. But the before and after are completely clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any memory lapses or gaps in your daily life where you couldn't recall what happened?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, just the time I lose when I'm actually frozen, like I mentioned. But other than that, no. My memory is completely fine. I don't have gaps in my day or forget conversations or anything like that. I pretty much remember everything normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or the world around you when you're not having one of these freezing episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's really just those times when I freeze up that are weird. Like my brain just kind of paused. \n\nI wasn't trying to memorize the numbers or anything like that, they were just coming out of my mouth. I didn't even realize I was stuck there doing it until my manager tapped my shoulder and asked if I was okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or anxious right before these episodes where you freeze up?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, the restaurant gets busy, sure, but I don't feel especially stressed or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really seem connected to that. \n\nIt's not like I'm overwhelmed by a big dinner rush or anything when it starts. But I'm always just in my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Like... I know exactly what I was doing right up until I pause. I'll remember walking over to a table or picking up a tray. And then, once someone snaps me out of it, I know exactly where I am and what's going on. I just... I guess I just lose the time while I'm actually frozen. But the before and after are completely clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any memory lapses or gaps in your daily life where you couldn't recall what happened?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, just the time I lose when I'm actually frozen, like I mentioned. But other than that, no. My memory is completely fine. I don't have gaps in my day or forget conversations or anything like that. I pretty much remember everything normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or the world around you when you're not having one of these freezing episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, when I'm not stuck like that, I feel completely normal. I don't feel disconnected or... or like things aren't real. I'm just going about my day, working, doing whatever I normally do. It's really just those times when I freeze up that are weird. But otherwise, no, I feel perfectly connected to everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a kind of trance when you're frozen like that?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it a trance, really. I just... I don't feel anything at all. It's like I'm just paused. I'm not daydreaming or zoned out in some other world, I'm just not there for a minute. Then someone touches my shoulder and I'm back. So no, not really a trance. Just a blank spot.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like things aren't real when you're not frozen?\n\nPatient: Um, no. Like I think I just mentioned a minute ago, I really don't. When I'm not having one of those frozen moments, everything is completely normal. Things feel real, and I feel like myself. I don't have any weird out-of-body feelings or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were in a different state of consciousness during one of these episodes where you froze up?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's... well, it's kind of like what I was just saying about the trance thing. I don't feel like my mind is somewhere else or in some altered state. It's really just a blank spot where I'm completely paused. So, no, I wouldn't call it a different state of consciousness.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced feeling as though you are observing yourself or your surroundings from a distance when you are not frozen?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um, it's really just like what I was saying a minute ago... I don't have any out-of-body feelings or anything like that. I'm definitely not watching myself from a distance. I haven't really noticed any kind of warning or anxious feeling beforehand.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything like that. It's more just... I'm there, and then suddenly I'm just kind of stuck, and I don't realize I'm stuck until someone snaps me out of it. But I'm always just in my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... it's not like I feel like someone else is making me do things or moving my body for me. When I get stuck like that at work, I'm just paused. I'm not doing things against my will or anything. I just kind of stop doing much of anything at all until someone gets my attention. So, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember everything that happens before and after one of these episodes where you freeze up?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I remember everything before and after. Like... I know exactly what I was doing right up until I pause. I'll remember walking over to a table or picking up a tray. And then, once someone snaps me out of it, I know exactly where I am and what's going on. I just... I guess I just lose the time while I'm actually frozen. But the before and after are completely clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any memory lapses or gaps in your daily life where you couldn't recall what happened?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, just the time I lose when I'm actually frozen, like I mentioned. But other than that, no. My memory is completely fine. I don't have gaps in my day or forget conversations or anything like that. I pretty much remember everything normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or the world around you when you're not having one of these freezing episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, when I'm not stuck like that, I feel completely normal. I don't feel disconnected or... or like things aren't real. I'm just going about my day, working, doing whatever I normally do. It's really just those times when I freeze up that are weird. But otherwise, no, I feel perfectly connected to everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a kind of trance when you're frozen like that?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it a trance, really. I just... I don't feel anything at all. It's like I'm just paused. I'm not daydreaming or zoned out in some other world, I'm just not there for a minute. Then someone touches my shoulder and I'm back. So no, not really a trance. Just a blank spot.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like things aren't real when you're not frozen?\n\nPatient: Um, no. Like I think I just mentioned a minute ago, I really don't. When I'm not having one of those frozen moments, everything is completely normal. Things feel real, and I feel like myself. I don't have any weird out-of-body feelings or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were in a different state of consciousness during one of these episodes where you froze up?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's... well, it's kind of like what I was just saying about the trance thing. I don't feel like my mind is somewhere else or in some altered state. It's really just a blank spot where I'm completely paused. So, no, I wouldn't call it a different state of consciousness.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced feeling as though you are observing yourself or your surroundings from a distance when you are not frozen?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um, it's really just like what I was saying a minute ago... I don't have any out-of-body feelings or anything like that. I'm definitely not watching myself from a distance. When I'm not frozen, everything is just completely normal and I'm right here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any emotions at all when you're frozen like that?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, like I was saying, it's really just a blank spot. I don't feel scared or sad or... anything at all, really. There's just nothing going on emotionally when it happens. It's just completely paused.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings when you're not having one of these freezing episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. When I'm not frozen, I feel pretty normal. I know what I'm thinking and feeling. It's really just those times when everything stops that are weird, but otherwise, I feel connected to myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF44.5, F43.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... things have been getting weird at work, and my boss said I needed to see someone. I'm a waitress, and lately I've just been... well, what scared everyone is that I started freezing up right in the middle of a shift. Like, I'd just stop moving. Or I'd do things totally out of order. The other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer, and I was just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. I didn't really realize I was doing it until they pointed it out, but it's been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember what was going through your mind when you were standing by the soda station holding the key to the cash drawer and repeating table numbers?\n\nPatient: Honestly... no, I don't. It's hard to explain, but I don't really remember thinking anything at all. It was just... blank, I guess? Like my brain just kind of paused. \n\nI wasn't trying to memorize the numbers or anything like that, they were just coming out of my mouth. I didn't even realize I was stuck there doing it until my manager tapped my shoulder and asked if I was okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or anxious right before these episodes where you freeze up?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, the restaurant gets busy, sure, but I don't feel especially stressed or anxious right before it happens. It doesn't really seem connected to that. \n\nIt's not like I'm overwhelmed by a big dinner rush or anything when it starts. I could just be doing my normal routine, walking to the back or grabbing a glass, and it just... happens. I haven't really noticed any kind of warning or anxious feeling beforehand.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything like that. It's more just... I'm there, and then suddenly I'm just kind of stuck, and I don't realize I'm stuck until someone snaps me out of it. But I'm always just in my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... it's not like I feel like someone else is making me do things or moving my body for me. When I get stuck like that at work, I'm just paused. I'm not doing things against my will or anything. I just kind of stop doing much of anything at all until someone gets my attention. So, no, I wouldn't say I feel out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember everything that happens before and after one of these episodes where you freeze up?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I remember everything before and after. Like... I know exactly what I was doing right up until I pause. I'll remember walking over to a table or picking up a tray. And then, once someone snaps me out of it, I know exactly where I am and what's going on. I just... I guess I just lose the time while I'm actually frozen. But the before and after are completely clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any memory lapses or gaps in your daily life where you couldn't recall what happened?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, just the time I lose when I'm actually frozen, like I mentioned. But other than that, no. My memory is completely fine. I don't have gaps in my day or forget conversations or anything like that. I pretty much remember everything normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel disconnected from yourself or the world around you when you're not having one of these freezing episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, when I'm not stuck like that, I feel completely normal. I don't feel disconnected or... or like things aren't real. I'm just going about my day, working, doing whatever I normally do. It's really just those times when I freeze up that are weird. But otherwise, no, I feel perfectly connected to everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a kind of trance when you're frozen like that?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it a trance, really. I just... I don't feel anything at all. It's like I'm just paused. I'm not daydreaming or zoned out in some other world, I'm just not there for a minute. Then someone touches my shoulder and I'm back. So no, not really a trance. Just a blank spot.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like things aren't real when you're not frozen?\n\nPatient: Um, no. Like I think I just mentioned a minute ago, I really don't. When I'm not having one of those frozen moments, everything is completely normal. Things feel real, and I feel like myself. I don't have any weird out-of-body feelings or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were in a different state of consciousness during one of these episodes where you froze up?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's... well, it's kind of like what I was just saying about the trance thing. I don't feel like my mind is somewhere else or in some altered state. It's really just a blank spot where I'm completely paused. So, no, I wouldn't call it a different state of consciousness.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced feeling as though you are observing yourself or your surroundings from a distance when you are not frozen?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um, it's really just like what I was saying a minute ago... I don't have any out-of-body feelings or anything like that. I'm definitely not watching myself from a distance. When I'm not frozen, everything is just completely normal and I'm right here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any emotions at all when you're frozen like that?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, like I was saying, it's really just a blank spot. I don't feel scared or sad or... anything at all, really. There's just nothing going on emotionally when it happens. It's just completely paused.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings when you're not having one of these freezing episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. When I'm not frozen, I feel pretty normal. I know what I'm thinking and feeling. Like I said, everything just stops. I'm not moving or doing any kind of tasks... it's just a complete pause. I'm just frozen.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're spaced out or disconnected from things when you're not frozen?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um... kind of like I mentioned a minute ago, when I'm not having one of those pauses, I feel fine. I don't really space out or lose touch with what's going on around me. I just go about my day, you know, working my shifts at the restaurant and everything. It's only when I freeze up that things stop.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
